   - R is connected to J and Q.

   - Path 2: S -> F -> J -> Q -> R

   - Path 4: S -> F -> B -> T -> J -> Q -> R
   - Path 5: S -> F -> T -> B -> T -> J -> Q -> R

1. **Node S** is directly connected to nodes A, B, and E.
2. **Node Q** is directly connected to nodes P and I.

We need to find a path from S to Q that minimizes the number of edges traversed.

### Step-by-Step Analysis:

1. **From Node S**:
   - S → A
   - S → B
   - S → E

2. **From Node Q**:
   - Q → P
   - Q → I

### Exploring Paths:

- **From S to E**:
  - E has connections to F and B.
  - F has connections to T.
  - T has no further connections that lead to Q.

- **From S to B**:
  - B has connections to A, F, T, E, and G.
  - G has connections to C, H, J, and O.
  - C has connections to N and R.
  - H has no further connections that lead to Q.
  - J has connections to I.
  - I is directly connected to Q.
  - Therefore, the path S → B → G → J → I → Q is a valid path.

- **From S to A**:
  - A has connections to E and B.
  - No new connections that lead to Q.

### Evaluating Path Lengths:

- Path 1: S → B → G → J → I → Q
  - Length: 5

### Conclusion:

The shortest path between the two black nodes S and Q is:

Length: 5, Path: [S, B, G, J, I, Q]

- **The shortest path found is: O -> B -> C -> G -> T -> F -> S -> E -> A**

- **Consider naively another concrete example from O to Q:** We trust closest neighbors tightly connect.

- **Verifying positional distance and the shortest traced path:** Direct Path confirm as B and F likely adding least nodes covering initial end J using Q or I, inspection yields the discrepancies.

1. **Identify the black nodes:** The two black nodes are I and Q.
2. **Identify immediate neighboring nodes:**
   - Node I is connected to: H, J, Q
   - Node Q is connected to: I, J, O, P

3. **Iteratively find other neighboring nodes:**
   - Node H is connected to: G, B, I
   - Node J is connected to: I, Q, R
   - Node O is connected to: Q, R
   - Node P is connected to: Q
   - Node G is connected to: H, C, B
   - Node B is connected to: G, H, C, S, A, E, F, T
   - Node R is connected to: J, O, C
   - Node C is connected to: R, G, B, N

4. **Analyze paths:**
   - From I to Q directly:
     - (I, Q)
   - From I to Q through H:
     - (I, H), (H, G), (G, B), (B, T), (T, F), (F, E), (E, S), (S, C), (C, R), (R, O), (O, Q)
   - From I to Q through J:
     - (I, J), (J, R), (R, C), (C, B), (B, T), (T, F), (F, E), (E, S), (S, C), (C, R), (R, O), (O, Q)
   - From I to Q through J directly:
     - (I, J), (J, Q)

5. **Evaluate the found paths:**
   - Removing edge (I, Q) disconnects the direct path but not all other paths.
   - Removing edge (J, Q) disconnects another direct path but not all other paths.
   - Paths through H, G, B, T, F, E, S, C, R, O are all interconnected. Removing these edges one by one shows that multiple edges need to be removed.

6. **Find the minimum cut set:**

By simplifing the paths and testing the removal of edges iteratively:

- Path I -> H -> G -> B -> T -> F -> E -> S -> C -> R -> O -> Q
- Path I -> J -> R -> O -> Q

We identify that to disconnect I and Q completely, we need to remove edges:

   - (H, G)
   - (J, R)

This ensures that no paths exist between I and Q, and it is the minimal set.

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(H, G), (J, R)].
